Let's devise practical search using high-dimensional optimize with FFT and multiplicative updates. We need ensure it can find below 1.5 from scratch. Start with smart initial patterns based on continuous half-wave combinations, then optimize.

Potential initial patterns (length n, normalized to mean 1):
- A0: uniform ones.
- A1: 1 + A*cos(π t) (half-wave: high left, low right or vice versa). Many phases: 1 + A*cos(π(t+phi)).
- A2: 1 + A*cos(2π t) (low ends high center etc).
- A3: sums: 1 + A1*cos(πt) + A2*cos(2πt) + A3*sin? plus smooth.
- A4: Beta/Polya distributions low/high: t^α(1-t)^β normalized; but sum? We can normalize mean1.
- Random smooth: inverse FFT low-frequency noise.

We can optimize each via local search. Use multiple lengths n: maybe 2048, 4096, 8192, 6733. Because final length can be chosen after best. For search, evaluate fast objective. Use length powers of two for FFT. If best at 8192, maybe final length 8192. But previous length 6733; maybe N=8192 exact evaluation O(67M) okay. Could also interpolate to 6733 to match? Not necessary.

However official target maybe known for length 6733? It says Length of construction: 6733 for previous. They may compare any length. Larger sequences better attack surface but too slow. Use 8192 maybe good. Could also final use best length among 4096, 6733, 8192.

Need implement propose_candidate within 2400s. We can use our own fast eval and maybe call evaluate_sequence for occasional exact check to avoid FFT roundoff and clipping. But if we don't call, final returned sequence will be evaluated externally. We need guarantee valid: list of floats, nonnegative, finite, not too large, sum>0.01. We can check ourselves. Function propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs). We can set seed and time. Need top-level helper functions no closures/lambdas.

Let's design functions:

- next_fast_len(n): maybe use scipy.fft.next_fast_len if available else power of 2. Since using 1 CPU, numpy power-of-two okay. Use 2**ceil(log2(n)). For n=8192, conv length 16383 -> N=16384. fast. For gradient with mask length 3n -> N=32768.

- fast_convolution(a): N=next_fast_len(2*len(a)-1); F=fft(a,N); c=ifft(F*F).real; return c[:2n-1]. Ensure nonnegative (small negative set0). For accuracy, maybe if n small use np.convolve? FFT error ~1e-12*n. Accept.

- fast_score(a): if any invalid return inf; c=fast_convolution(a); S=sum; if S<0.01 return inf; maxc=max(c); return 2*len(a)*maxc/(S*S). But in search we normalize sum to n, so score=2*maxc/n. Keep scale maybe.

- normalize_sum(a, target): s=sum; if s<=0 return zeros; return a*target/s. Maybe clip nonneg before.

- objective_and_grad(a, tau, top_frac=...): Given a normalized sum n (target_n). c=conv. Compute weights w. To avoid overflow, shift c by max. Use tau. If tau <=0: hard active set: maxc=max; w = ones where c>=maxc - tol / count. If soft: logs = (c-max)/tau; w=exp(logs); w/=sum. Then gradient d = 2 * convolve a with reversed w as derived. We need implement for full mask length L. But if tau large, w length L. FFT Ngrad=next_fast_len(n+L-1)=next_fast_len(3n-2). Use ifft(fft(a,Ngrad)*fft(w_rev,Ngrad)); d = h[L-1-n+1? indices]. Let's verify index.
We define h = conv_full(a, w_rev). a len n, w_rev len L=2n-1. h length n+L-1=3n-2. h[p]=∑_{r=0}^{n-1} a[r] w_rev[p-r] =∑ a[r] w[(L-1)-(p-r)] =∑ a[r] w[r + (L-1-p)]. d_i =∑_r a[r] w[i+r] for i+r ≤ L-1. Choose p=L-1-i. Then d_i = h[p] = h[L-1-i]. Indices i=0..n-1 -> p=L-1,2... So d = h[L-1:0:-1]. Good. Factor 2.
But w sums to 1 and represents active weight. Gradient of average active d. If objective logsumexp, gradient = d/tau. We'll return d * scale maybe.

If active set small, can compute direct faster: for max active indices, for each k, add a[max(0,k-n+1):min(n,k+1)] to d shifted. But FFT O(n log n) fine. Ngrad ~4n, with n=8192 32768, a few thousand iterations okay. Could be bottleneck. We can optimize by computing gradient direct for hard active if count small. Soft all weights requires FFT. Maybe use top K weights (sparse) and direct. Let's implement both.

- gradient_direct(a, w, indices): If len(indices)*min(avg window) less than n log n. For each k, start=max(0,k-n+1), stop=min(n,k+1). d[start:stop] += w_k * a[start:stop? Wait d_i contribution for k active: a_{k-i} for i from max(0,k-n+1) to min(n-1,k). The array slice d[start:stop] where i=start..stop-1. Values a_{k-i}. For i=start..stop-1, k-i ranges k-start down to k-stop+1. Need reversed slice of a. e.g., if k=3,n=5, valid i=0..3, a[3-i]=a[3],a[2],a[1],a[0]. d[0:4] += w * a[3::-1]. So d[start:stop] += w * a[k-start: k-stop:-1]? Need generic. Simpler: vals = a[max(0,k-stop+1): k-start+1][::-1]. Or allocate slice. For performance, loop over top K small. Could be okay for 100 indices. For soft all, FFT.

Maybe use hard active set (top K) for gradient to focus max. K can be e.g. max(1, int(0.01*n)) or based on coefficient threshold. Then direct. This is efficient. But exact max may be single ridge; use top K to smooth.

- update_multiplicative(a, grad, step, noise): We want reduce objective. a_new = a * exp(-step*grad + noise). Clip >0, normalize sum to target. If grad has large mean, exp scaling overall irrelevant after normalization. We can center grad to reduce degeneration: grad_c = grad - mean(grad). Because sum normalization removes constant but multiplicative with constant also removed after normalization (a e^{-ηc}/sum unchanged). So can center for numerical stability. grad_c = grad - mean. Then log_factor = -eta*grad_c + entropy/rng noise. Need avoid overflow: clip log_factor to [-5,5]. a *= exp(log_factor), a[a<0]=0, normalize.

Gradient sign: For minimizing max, if a_i participates in high c (large grad), reduce. So log_factor = -eta grad_c. If grad positive high, decrease. However if grad low negative after center, increase. Good.

Step size: Need adapt. We can try Armijo: compute score before, apply step, if not finite or score too high, reduce step. But each score requires conv. Since we already have grad from current c, to evaluate new we need conv. We can accept if score lower or if using smooth objective. Could do multiple step retries. For efficiency, choose fixed step with phase-in.

Use Adam? State m,v for grad_centered. Update z? If a direct, maintain m,v length n, O(n). Step per iteration: FFT conv (N=16384), weight, gradient (if direct topK maybe 1K ops), update. Could do ~5-20k iterations for n=8192 in 2400s. Enough.

Fast convolution:
```
def fast_convolution(a):
    n=len(a)
    N=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N, n)  # returns first n? Actually irfft with n maybe truncates to first n complex? We need 2n-2.
```
Better: `c = np.fft.ifft(fa*fa).real[:2*n-1]` using full complex if not rfft. rfft returns N/2+1; product; irfft returns length N. Use `np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, N)[:2*n-1]`. fa*fa should be real-ish; due numerical. N power of 2. For n=8192, N=16384. Good.
Need ensure small negative due roundoff: `c[c<0]=0` maybe before max. But if negative tiny, max unaffected. Use `np.maximum(c,0)`.

Gradient weights function:
Option A: Softmax weights and FFT convolution with reversed weights.
```
def gradient_from_weights(a,w):
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    N=1 << (n+L-1).bit_length()
    aw=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    wr=np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], N)
    h=np.fft.irfft(aw*wr, N)
    d=h[L-1:L-1-n:-1]  # length n reversed
    return 2*d
```
Need verify h length N, we use indices L-1 down to L-n. For i=0 -> p=L-1; i=n-1 -> p=L-n. yes.
Potential issue w from softmax sum maybe 1; if w all positive. If tau small and max huge, weights sparse; FFT still okay.

```
def make_weights(c, tau=None, top_frac=0.02):
    maxc=c.max()
    if top_frac is None:
       # softmax: shift
       if tau <=0: ...
       logs=(c-maxc)/tau
       # subtract max logs already 0; exp; sum
       w=np.exp(logs); w/=w.sum()
       # If effective count small? set tiny to0? Could keep.
       active = w > 1e-12
       if active.sum() < max(1,n//50): return top? 
    # hard: topK = max(1,int(round(top_frac*n?))) but c length L~2n; maybe K=max(1,int(0.01*L))
```

Direct gradient for top K:
```
def gradient_topK(a, c, K):
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    vals, idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]  # top K
    # maybe sort descending not needed
    d=np.zeros(n)
    # For numerical, uniform weights
    invK=1.0/K
    for k in idx:
        start=max(0,k-(n-1))
        stop=min(n,k+1)
        # slice d[start:stop] += invK * a[k-start : k-stop+1 : -1]? Let's derive.
        # i from start to stop-1, value a[k-i]. k-i ranges k-start down to k-stop+1.
        # If k-start >= k-stop+1. step -1.
        s_start = k - (stop-1)  # min index in a for value?
        s_end = k - start + 1  # max index exclusive?
        # Want a[s_start : s_end][::-1]
        # Example k=3,n=5 start0 stop4 => s_start=3-3=0, s_end=3-0+1=4 -> a[0:4][::-1]=[a3,a2,a1,a0] good.
        d[start:stop] += invK * a[s_start:s_end][::-1]
    return 2.0*d
```
But argpartition on c length L each iteration O(L) okay. K small. If K=1, direct O(n).
Could also use weighted top with weights softmax among top K to smooth. But uniform top OK.

Update multiplicative with gradient:
```
def multiplicative_update(a, grad, eta, rng, noise=0.0):
    g = grad - np.mean(grad)
    # scale to std maybe 1 to make eta meaningful
    scale = np.max(np.abs(g))
    if scale > 0: g = g / scale
    logf = -eta * g
    if noise: logf += rng.normal(0, noise, len(a))
    # clip to avoid under/overflow and extreme zero
    logf = np.clip(logf, -3.0, 3.0)
    a2 = a * np.exp(logf)
    a2 = np.clip(a2, 0.0, None)
    s=a2.sum()
    if s <=0: return a
    return (a2 * (target/s))
```
If gradient has mean, centering. But in multiplicative normalization, constant factor irrelevant; centering optional. If grad all same (uniform max), update no change. Good.

Step eta: The scaled gradient max=1, eta controls log change. Try eta in [0.05, 0.5]. Too large may oscillate. Adaptive:
```
if new_score < old_score*(1-1e-12): accept; eta = min(eta*1.1, 2.0)
elif new_score < old_score*(1+temp): accept; eta maybe same
else: reject; eta *=0.7
```
Because scaled gradient each step, objective change ~? We can use armijo: if new_score <= old_score + eps? For smooth objective, but okay.
Maybe use deterministic line search: Try eta, if not improvement, reduce; after accept, increase.

def make_basis(n, K):
    # DCT-II normalized zero-mean basis (exclude DC)
    t = np.arange(n)
    phi = np.empty((K,n), float)
    for m in range(1,K+1):
       phi[m-1] = np.cos(np.pi * m * (t+0.5)/n)
    # Also include sine? DCT basis may not span all if only cos; use both cos and sin for flexibility.

def normalize_sequence(a, target_sum, max_value=1000.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a,0.0,max_value)
    s = a.sum()

Fast score:
```
def fast_score_array(a):
    a = np.asarray(a)
    if a.size==0 or not finite: inf
    a = np.clip(a,0,1000)
    S = a.sum()
    if S < 0.01: return inf
    c = fast_convolution(a)
    return float(2.0*a.size*np.max(c)/(S*S))
```
For search normalized target, can use `fast_score_normalized(a,n)` but use general for safety. `fast_convolution` returns nonnegative.

`fast_convolution` should accept list/array; ensure contiguous float.

```
def fast_convolution(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.empty(0)
    L=2*n-1
    N=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:L]
    # remove tiny negative
    c = c.real
    np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    return c
```
For self convolution, fa*fa (not abs square). If fa complex, product may have small imaginary? irfft expects Hermitian; product of rfft of real with itself is not necessarily Hermitian? Wait rfft returns one-sided spectrum of real signal. The product fa*fa comp corresponds to circular convolution of a with a? For real a, `irfft(fa*fa)` gives real sequence? The Fourier transform of circular convolution is product of transforms. Since linear convolution zero-padded length N, circular length N equals linear if N>=L. The transform of real a is Hermitian; product of two Hermitian is Hermitian, so irfft valid. Good.

Gradient weights via FFT:
```
def gradient_from_weights(a, w):
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    N=1 << (n + L - 2 -1).bit_length()? n+L-1 =3n-2, length. Full convolution length = n + L -1 =3n-2.
    N=1 << (n+L-2).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], N)
    h=np.fft.irfft(fa*fw,N)
    # length h N; need indices from L-1 down to L-n
    d=h[L-1:L-n-1:-1]
    return 2.0*d
```
Check slice: Python h[start:stop:-1] stops before stop. If L-1 down to L-n inclusive, stop = L-n-1. Use `h[L-1:L-n-1:-1]`. For n=1: L=1, N>=? n+L-1=1. stop= -1? h[0: -1:-1]? Hmm n=1 not used. For n>1. Let's test n=5 L=9, want p=8,7,6,5,4. stop=9-5-1=3 => h[8:3:-1] gives 8..4. good.
If n=2 L=3, want 2,1; stop=3-2-1=0 => h[2:0:-1] gives 2,1.
Good.

Could use direct top K:
```
def gradient_topK(a, c, K):
    n=len(a); K = min(K, 2*n-1)
    if K <=0: return np.zeros(n)
    maxc=np.max(c)
    if K == 1:
       k = int(np.argmax(c)); return _grad_single(a,k)
    idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
    d=np.zeros(n)
    inv=1.0/K
    for k in idx:
       start = max(0, k-(n-1))
       stop = min(n, k+1)
       s_start = k - (stop - 1)
       s_end = k - start + 1
       d[start:stop] += inv * a[s_start:s_end][::-1]
    return 2.0*d

def _grad_single(a,k):
    n=len(a)
    start=max(0,k-n+1); stop=min(n,k+1)
    d=np.zeros(n)
    s_start=k-(stop-1); s_end=k-start+1
    d[start:stop]=a[s_start:s_end][::-1]
    return 2*d
```
Need ensure s_start nonnegative? Example k=0: start0 stop1, s_start=0, s_end=1, good. k=2n-2: start=n-1,stop=n, s_start=2n-2-(n-1)=n-1, s_end=2n-2-(n-1)+1=n, a[n-1:n]. good.

Weights for smooth gradient maybe not needed if top K. But low-dim L-BFGS uses weights softmax. Implement `softmax_weights(c, tau)` with upper bounds:
```
def softmax_weights(c, tau):
    cmax=np.max(c)
    logs=(c-cmax)/tau
    # To avoid underflow, subtract max (already 0); exp
    w=np.exp(logs)
    s=w.sum()
    if s==0 or not finite: hard top
    return w/s
```
For tau very small many underflow; okay. Use `tau = max(1e-6, float(tau))`.

Low-dim L-BFGS objective:
```
def fourier_objgrad(p, n, basis, tau, target=None):
    if target is None: target=float(n)
    z = np.dot(p, basis)
    mx = z.max()
    e = np.exp(z - mx)
    a = e / e.sum()
    a *= target
    c=fast_convolution(a)
    cmax=c.max()
    logs=(c-cmax)/tau
    w=np.exp(logs); w/=w.sum()
    logZ = cmax + np.log(w.sum() before normalized?)
```

```

Z = w_un.sum()

Basis construction:
```
def make_basis(n, n_freq=12, n_poly=12):
    t = np.linspace(0,1,n) if n>1 else np.array([0.])
    funcs=[]
    # Chebyshev T_m(2t-1) for m=1..n_poly
    x=2*t-1
    for m in range(1,n_poly+1):
       tm, tm1 = x, 1.0
       if m==1: pass else iterative
       funcs.append(tm)
    # Fourier cos/sin m=1..n_freq
    for m in range(1,n_freq+1):
       funcs.append(np.cos(2*np.pi*m*t))
       funcs.append(np.sin(2*np.pi*m*t))
    phi=np.array(funcs)
    # Center rows (remove mean) to avoid constant
    phi = phi - phi.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)
    # Optional orthonormalize? not needed; standardize scale to unit norm
    norms=np.linalg.norm(phi,axis=1)
    norms[norms<1e-12]=1
    phi /= norms
    return phi
```
K = n_poly+n_freq*2 maybe 36. L-BFGS with 36 dims okay. Maybe too many; reduce for speed: n_poly=8, n_freq=8 ->24. We'll choose 24.

Two-block start generation:
```
def make_two_block(n, frac, ratio):
   m = max(1, min(n-1, int(round(frac*n))))
   # ratio A/B = ratio
   # sum: m A + (n-m) B =n, A=ratio B => B = n/(m*ratio+n-m)
   B = n / (m*ratio + (n-m))
   A = ratio*B
   a=np.empty(n); a[:m]=A; a[m:]=B
   return normalize_sequence(a,n)
```
Search over frac 0.05..0.5 and ratio 0.2..5. Evaluate fast. Use maybe random 200 combinations.

def random_block_sequence(n, rng, nblocks=8):
    edges = sorted([0, n, *rng.integers(1,n-1,nblocks-1)])
    vals = rng.normal(1,0.5,nblocks) clip min0.1
    a=np.repeat(vals, edges[1:]-[:-1]); normalize
```

def upsample_sequence(seq, n):
    xold=np.linspace(0,1,len(seq)); xnew=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    # interpolate log a to preserve positive? Use a directly, nonnegative
    out=np.interp(xnew,xold,seq)
    return normalize_sequence(out,n)
```

Let's write local_search robust:
```
def local_search(a, budget_s, seed, label, verbose=True):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start=time.monotonic()
    n=len(a)
    target=float(n)
    a=normalize_sequence(a.copy(), target)
    c=fast_convolution(a)
    score=2*n*c.max()/(a.sum()**2) # a sum target
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score
    eta=0.25; accept_rel=0.02; noise=0.02
    no_improve=0
    iter=0
    L=2*n-1
    # initial K frac maybe 0.03
    while time.monotonic() < start+budget_s:
       iter +=1
       K_frac = max(0.002, 0.05*(1.0 - min(iter/5000,1)))? But if budget longer, progress.
       K = max(1, int(K_frac * L))
       # If K too many (precision), cap 2000
       K = min(K, 2000)
       grad = gradient_topK(a,c,K)
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)): reset
       g = grad - np.mean(grad)
       scale = np.max(np.abs(g))
       if scale < 1e-15: break
       g /= scale
       logf = -eta * g
       if noise > 1e-6: logf += rng.normal(0, noise, n)
       logf = np.clip(logf, -3, 3)
       cand = a * np.exp(logf)
       cand = normalize_sequence(cand, target)
       # If cand max too high or many zeros, blend with a
       if cand.max() > 1000:
           frac = 1000 / cand.max()
           cand = np.clip(cand,0,1000)
           cand = normalize_sequence(cand, target)
       cand_c = fast_convolution(cand)
       cand_score = 2*n*cand_c.max()/(cand.sum()**2)
       if cand_score < best_score:
           best=cand.copy(); best_score=cand_score; no_improve=0
           if verbose and best_score < ...: print
       if cand_score < score * (1.0 + accept_rel) + 1e-15:
           a=cand; c=cand_c; score=cand_score
           if cand_score < score_old*(1-1e-10): eta=min(eta*1.08,1.2); accept_rel=max(0.001, accept_rel*0.995); noise=max(0.005, noise*0.995)
           else: eta*=0.95
           no_improve +=1
           if no_improve > 800:
               # perturb best/current to escape
               a = smooth_perturb(best, rng, 0.35)
               cand? compute c,score
               eta=0.3; accept_rel=0.05; noise=0.05; no_improve=0
       else:
           eta = max(0.01, eta*0.7)
           if eta <=0.011:
               accept_rel = max(0.005, accept_rel*0.9)
               noise = max(0.01, noise*0.9)
               eta=0.3
               no_improve +=1
               if no_improve > 1500: perturb
    return best,best_score
```
Need avoid `score_old` bug. We'll implement clean.

def _pow2(x):

`normalize_sequence`:
```
def normalize_sequence(a, target_sum, max_value=1000.0):
    a = np.array(a, dtype=np.float64, copy=True)
    if a.size == 0: return a
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, max_value)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        a.fill(target_sum / a.size)
        return np.clip(a,0,max_value)
    a *= target_sum / s
    a = np.clip(a,0,max_value)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-15:
        a.fill(target_sum/a.size)
    else:
        a *= target_sum/s
        a = np.clip(a,0,max_value)
        # if max > max_value due rare, scale extra component? 
        if a.max() > max_value:
            # reduce large and add to rest to keep sum
            excess = (a - max_value).sum()
            a = np.clip(a,0,max_value)
            rest = a.sum()
            if rest < target_sum:
                # distribute to elements below max
                mask = a < max_value
                if mask.any():
                    a[mask] += (target_sum - rest)/mask.sum()
                    a = np.clip(a,0,max_value)
                    # final rescale if needed
                    s2=a.sum()
                    if s2>0: a *= target_sum/s2
                    a=np.clip(a,0,max_value)
    # enforce min tiny? not always to preserve zeros; maybe add 0
    return a
```
But scaling after clip can again max; loop not. Given search avoids, okay.

`make_harmonic_sequences(n, rng, num)`:
```
def make_harmonic_sequence(n, rng, max_freq=6, max_amp=2.0):
   t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   z=np.zeros(n)
   for m in range(1,max_freq+1):
      amp = rng.uniform(-max_amp, max_amp)/(m+1)**0.7
      ph = rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
      z += amp * np.cos(2*np.pi*m*t + ph)
   # add half-wave
   amp = rng.uniform(-max_amp, max_amp)
   z += amp * np.cos(np.pi*(2*t-1))  # Chebyshev T1
   a = np.exp(z - z.max())
   return normalize_sequence(a,n)
```
`make_block_sequence`:
```
def make_block_sequence(n, rng, num_blocks=8):
   edges = np.sort(np.concatenate(([0,n], rng.integers(1,n-1,num_blocks-1))))
   lengths = np.diff(edges)
   vals = np.maximum(rng.normal(1.0,0.7,num_blocks),0.1)
   # maybe set some zero
   if rng.random()<0.2: vals[rng.integers(num_blocks)] = 0.0
   a=np.repeat(vals, lengths.astype(int))
   return normalize_sequence(a,n)
```
If zeros, normalize may scale. If all zero due clip, avoid.

`smooth_perturbation`:
```
def smooth_perturbation(a, rng, strength=0.3):
   n=len(a)
   t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   z=np.zeros(n)
   for m in range(1,5):
       amp=strength/(0.5+m*0.5)
       ph=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
       z += amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*t+ph)
   # add random sparse blocks maybe
   out=a*np.exp(z)
   if rng.random()<0.3:
      width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//200), max(2,n//20)))
      start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
      out[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,strength,width))
   return normalize_sequence(out, n)
```

`local_search` detailed:

Pseudo robust:
```
def local_search(a, budget_s, seed=0, label=""):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0=time.monotonic()
    deadline=t0+budget_s
    n=len(a); target=float(n)
    a=normalize_sequence(a.copy(), target)
    c=fast_convolution(a)
    score=2*n*c.max()/(a.sum()**2) if a.sum()>0 else inf
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score
    eta=0.25; mom=0.0; accept_rel=0.03; noise=0.02; beta=0.35
    stagnant=0; it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       it+=1
       # If current invalid restore best
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)) or a.sum()<=0:
          a=best.copy(); c=fast_convolution(a); score=best_score
       L=2*n-1
       frac = max(0.002, 0.04/(1.0+it/2000.0))
       K = int(L*frac)
       K = max(1,min(K, max(4, min(800, n//8))))
       grad=gradient_topK(a,c,K)
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
          break
       g = grad - np.mean(grad)
       scale = np.max(np.abs(g))
       if scale < 1e-14:
          stagnant +=1
          if stagnant>50: break
          continue
       g /= scale
       mom = beta*mom + (1.0-beta)*g
       logf = -eta*mom
       if noise > 1e-6:
          logf += rng.normal(0.0, noise, n)
       logf = np.clip(logf, -3.0, 3.0)
       cand = a * np.exp(logf)
       cand = np.maximum(cand, 0.0)
       cand = normalize_sequence(cand, target)
       if cand.max() > 1000.0:
           cand = 0.5*cand + 0.5*best  # or restore
           cand = normalize_sequence(cand, target)
       c2 = fast_convolution(cand)
       cand_score = 2.0*n*c2.max()/(cand.sum()**2)
       if not np.isfinite(cand_score):
           eta=max(0.01,eta*0.7); continue
       if cand_score < best_score:
           best=cand.copy(); best_score=cand_score; stagnant=0
       if cand_score <= score*(1.0+accept_rel) + 1e-14:
           old=score
           a=cand; c=c2; score=cand_score; mom *=0.8
           if cand_score < old*(1.0-1e-10):
              eta = min(1.0, eta*1.07)
              accept_rel = max(0.001, accept_rel*0.998)
              noise = max(0.002, noise*0.998)
           else:
              eta = max(0.03, eta*0.96)
           stagnant +=1
       else:
           eta = max(0.01, eta*0.7)
           mom *=0.5
           stagnant +=1
       if stagnant > 800:
           # escape
           a = smooth_perturbation(best, rng, 0.35)
           c=fast_convolution(a); score=2.0*n*c.max()/(a.sum()**2)
           if not np.isfinite(score) or score > best_score*(1.05):
               a=best.copy(); c=fast_convolution(a); score=best_score
           eta=0.3; accept_rel=0.05; noise=0.05; mom=np.zeros(n); stagnant=0
    return best,best_score
```
Potential inefficiency: `norm` of c2 score uses cand.sum ~n. OK.

Pseudo propose:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+max(0.0,budget_s)-6.0
    final_n = 8192 if budget_s > 900 else 4096
    # Maybe choose 6733? Use 8192.
    global best_list best_score
    # Small phase if time
    n_small = 1024
    small_best = None
    if deadline - now > 240:
       budget_small = min(180, (deadline-now)*0.15)
       starts_small = generate_starts(n_small, budget=budget_small/2, rng=rng)
       for s in starts_small top3:
           b,sc = local_search(s, budget=budget_small/len(...), seed=...)
           if sc < best_small: ...
       # upsample small to final and include in global pool
    # Final starts
    starts_final = generate_starts(final_n, budget=..., rng=rng, additional=[upsampled_small])
    # Quick refine each top5 for 90s or budget proportion
    for seq in top starts:
       b,sc=local_search(seq, budget=quick_budget)
       update global
    # Long refine global best for remaining *0.7
    budget_long = max(0, deadline-now - reserve_exact)
    b,sc=local_search(global_best, budget=budget_long, seed=...)
    update global
    # exact evaluation top candidates
    reserve_exact = 30s
    candidates top from phases
    for cand in top:
       if time > reserve: break
       try exact = evaluate_sequence(cand.tolist())
       except: exact=inf
       if finite and < best_exact: update
    # final clip/normalize and valid fallback
    return [float(x) for x in best_seq]
```

def make_group_sequence(q, lengths):
   expq=np.exp(q-q.max())
   vals = expq / (lengths @ expq) * n
   a=np.repeat(vals, lengths)
   return a
def group_objgrad(q, lengths, n, tau):
   a=make_group_sequence(...)
   c=fast_convolution(a)
   cmax=c.max(); logs=(c-cmax)/tau; w_un=exp(logs); Zsum=sum; logZ=...
   grad_a=gradient_from_weights(a,w)/tau
   m=np.dot(grad_a,a)/n
   grad_z=a*(grad_a-m)
   # group q derivative: sum over indices in group
   grad_q=np.add.reduceat(grad_z, edges) maybe

   edges = np.zeros(G+1); edges[-1]=n
   if mode==0: edges = np.linspace(0,n,G+1); lengths = diff round ensuring sum.
   elif mode==random: edges = np.sort(rng.integers(1,n-1,G-1)); insert.
   elif beta: t = np.linspace(0,1,G+1); t = t**alpha; edges = t*n.
   lengths = np.diff(edges).astype(int)
   # fix if any zero: redistribute

def optimize_grouped_lengths(n,G,lengths,tau,budget,seed):
   q=rng.normal(0,0.3,G)
   m=np.zeros(G); v=np.zeros(G)
   step=0.1; best_q=q; obj=...
   while time<deadline:
      obj,grad=group_objgrad(q,lengths,n,tau)
      if finite and obj<best: update
      # center q? gradient maybe has null; subtract mean of grad weighted? Actually gradient wrt q has property ∑ P_g grad_g? For additive update, can project grad to sum? q + const null; center q after update.
      norm = np.linalg.norm(grad)
      if norm==0 break
      m = 0.9*m + 0.1*grad
      v = 0.99*v + 0.01*grad*grad
      q_new = q - step * m/(sqrt(v)+1e-8)
      # optional clip q to [-3,3]
      q_new = np.clip(q_new, -3,3)
      q_new = q_new - q_new.mean()
      obj_new,grad_new=group_objgrad(q_new,...)
      if obj_new < obj or obj_new < obj*1.05: accept; step*=1.05
      else reject; step*=0.7
```

`normalize_sequence` uses a while to clip/rescale maybe:
```
def normalize_sequence(a, target_sum, max_value=1000.0):
    a = np.array(a, dtype=np.float64, copy=True)
    if a.size == 0: return a
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, max_value)
    for _ in range(3):
        s = float(a.sum())
        if s <= 1e-15:
            a.fill(target_sum / a.size)
            a = np.clip(a,0,max_value)
            return a
        a *= target_sum / s
        a = np.clip(a,0,max_value)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        a.fill(target_sum/a.size)
    else:
        a *= target_sum/s
        a = np.clip(a,0,max_value)
    return a
```
If after last clip sum < target, but okay. Could final if max>max_value by scaling down and adding? Hard. But search avoids.

`fast_convolution`:
```
def fast_convolution(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    L = 2*n - 1
    N = 1 << (L - 1).bit_length()
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[:L]
    c = np.ascontiguousarray(c)
    np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    return c
```
If L=1, N=1, bit_length 0? (L-1=0).bit_length()=0 -> 1<<0=1 good.

`fast_score_array`:
```
def fast_score_array(a):
    a=np.asarray(a)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return inf
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<0.01: return inf
    c=fast_convolution(a)
    if c.size==0: return inf
    return float(2.0*n*float(np.max(c))/(s*s))
```
`inf` as `float('inf')` no math.inf? use `np.inf` but Python float. Use `float('inf')`.

`gradient_topK`:
```
def gradient_topK(a, c, K):
    n=a.size
    L=2*n-1
    K = max(1, min(int(K), L))
    d=np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if K == 1:
       k=int(np.argmax(c))
       start=max(0,k-n+1); stop=min(n,k+1)
       s_start=k-(stop-1); s_end=k-start+1
       d[start:stop]=a[s_start:s_end][::-1]
       return 2.0*d
    # If K large, maybe use argpartition; if K==L all? unlikely
    if K >= L:
       idx=np.arange(L)
    else:
       idx=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
    inv=1.0/float(K)
    for k in idx:
       k=int(k)
       start=max(0,k-(n-1))
       stop=min(n,k+1)
       s_start=k-(stop-1)
       s_end=k-start+1
       d[start:stop] += inv * a[s_start:s_end][::-1]
    return 2.0*d
```
If idx includes duplicates? argpartition no. For K=L, all.

`make_two_block`:
```
def make_two_block(n, frac, ratio):
    m = int(round(frac * n))
    if m <1: m=1
    if m >= n: m=n-1
    denom = m*ratio + (n-m)
    if denom <=0: B=1.0
    else: B = float(n) / denom
    A = ratio * B
    a=np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[:m]=A; a[m:]=B
    return normalize_sequence(a, float(n))
```
If ratio huge, A>1000? normalize clips; but B then sum changes; however make sequence then normalize clips; if A clips, sum target not exact but normalize again. Good. Could restrict ratio in generate.

`make_harmonic_sequence`:
```
def make_harmonic_sequence(n, rng, max_freq=6, max_amp=2.0):
    if n<2: return np.ones(n)
    t = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    z = np.zeros(n)
    for m in range(1, max_freq+1):
        amp = rng.uniform(-max_amp, max_amp) / ((m + 1.0) ** 0.7)
        ph = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0*np.pi)
        z += amp * np.cos(2.0*np.pi*m*t + ph)
    amp = rng.uniform(-max_amp, max_amp)
    z += amp * np.cos(np.pi*(2.0*t - 1.0))
    z -= z.max()
    a = np.exp(z)
    return normalize_sequence(a, float(n))
```
If amp zero, uniform.

`make_block_sequence` with random boundaries:
```
def make_block_sequence(n, rng, num_blocks=8):
    num_blocks=max(2,min(num_blocks,n//2))
    if n < 4: return normalize_sequence(np.ones(n), float(n))
    inner = rng.integers(1,n-1,num_blocks-1)
    edges = np.concatenate((np.array([0],dtype=int), np.sort(inner), np.array([n],dtype=int)))
    lengths = np.diff(edges).astype(int)
    # ensure positive
    lengths[lengths<=0]=1
    if lengths.sum()!=n: lengths[-1]+=n-lengths.sum(); if lengths[-1]<=0: lengths[:] = n//num_blocks...
    vals = rng.uniform(0.1,3.0,num_blocks)
    if rng.random()<0.2:
       vals[rng.integers(num_blocks)] = 0.0
    a=np.repeat(vals, lengths)
    return normalize_sequence(a, float(n))
```
Need handle negative length if adjustment makes last negative. Simpler: if any zero, use equal lengths.
```
if np.any(lengths <=0): edges=np.linspace(0,n,num_blocks+1); lengths=...
```
Use `np.linspace` floats round:
```
edges = np.round(np.linspace(0,n,num_blocks+1)).astype(int)
edges[0]=0; edges[-1]=n
lengths=np.diff(edges); lengths[lengths<=0]=1
```
Good.

`upsample_sequence`:
```
def upsample_sequence(seq, n):
    seq=np.asarray(seq,dtype=np.float64)
    m=seq.size
    if m==0: return normalize_sequence(np.ones(n),float(n))
    if m==n: return normalize_sequence(seq,float(n))
    xold=np.linspace(0,1,m)
    xnew=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    out=np.interp(xnew,xold,seq)
    return normalize_sequence(out,float(n))
```

`smooth_perturbation`:
```
def smooth_perturbation(a, rng, strength=0.35):
    n=a.size
    out=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if n>2:
       t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
       z=np.zeros(n)
       for m in range(1,4):
          amp=strength/(0.5+0.5*m)
          ph=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
          z += amp*np.cos(2π*m*t+ph)
       out *= np.exp(z)
    if rng.random()<0.35 and n>4:
       width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//200), max(2,n//10+1)))
       start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1))
       out[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,strength,width))
    return normalize_sequence(out,float(n))
```
Use `2.0*math.pi` or np.pi.

def sanitize_for_return(seq):

    _, seq = top_candidates[i]

        ex = float(evaluate_sequence(seq.tolist()))
    except Exception:
        ex = float('inf')
    if np.isfinite(ex) and ex < exact_best_score:
        exact_best_seq=seq; exact_best_score=ex
        print
```

def sanitize_for_return(seq):
    seq=np.asarray(seq,dtype=np.float64)
    if seq.size==0: return [1.0]
    seq=np.where(np.isfinite(seq), seq, 0.0)
    seq=np.clip(seq,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(seq.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
       return [1.0] * 1024
    # scale to mean 1 if possible
    scale = float(seq.size) / s
    if seq.max() * scale <= 1000.0:
       seq = seq * scale
    else:
       seq = np.clip(seq,0,1000)
    s=seq.sum()
    if s <0.01:
       return [1.0] * 1024
    return [float(x) for x in seq]
```

```python
"""
Searches for non-negative step-function heights minimizing
2*n*max(convolve(a,a))/(sum(a))^2.

Strategy:
- Use FFT-based convolution for fast objective/gradient evaluation.
- Run a coarse-to-fine multi-resolution local search over sequence length.
- Generate diverse starts: two-block, random blocks, harmonic/simple curves.
- Refine with projected multiplicative gradient steps on a top-peak active set.
- Keep a small candidate pool and validate the best candidates with the exact
  evaluator before returning.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math


def _pair_score(pair):
    return pair[0]


def normalize_sequence(a, target_sum, max_value=1000.0):
    """Clip, normalize to target sum, and keep values in [0, max_value]."""
    a = np.array(a, dtype=np.float64, copy=True)
    if a.size == 0:
        return a

    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, max_value)

    for _ in range(3):
        s = float(a.sum())
        if s <= 1e-15:
            a.fill(float(target_sum) / float(a.size))
            return np.clip(a, 0.0, max_value)
        a *= float(target_sum) / s
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, max_value)

    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        a.fill(float(target_sum) / float(a.size))
    else:
        a *= float(target_sum) / s
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, max_value)

    return a


def fast_convolution(a):
    """FFT-based linear convolution a * a, truncated to length 2n-1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    L = 2 * n - 1
    N = 1 << (L - 1).bit_length()
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[:L]
    c = np.ascontiguousarray(c)
    np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    return c


def fast_score_array(a):
    """Fast FFT-based analogue of evaluate_sequence()."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")

    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        return float("inf")

    c = fast_convolution(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")

    return float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(c)) / (s * s))


def gradient_topK(a, c, K):
    """
    Subgradient of the average maximum convolution coefficient.

    For active coefficient k, d c_k / d a_i = 2*a_{k-i} when valid.
    This returns 2 * average over top K coefficients.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    L = 2 * n - 1
    K = max(1, min(int(K), L))
    d = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    if K == 1:
        k = int(np.argmax(c))
        start = max(0, k - (n - 1))
        stop = min(n, k + 1)
        s_start = k - (stop - 1)
        s_end = k - start + 1
        d[start:stop] = a[s_start:s_end][::-1]
        return 2.0 * d

    if K >= L:
        idx = np.arange(L)
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]

    inv = 1.0 / float(K)
    for k in idx:
        k = int(k)
        start = max(0, k - (n - 1))
        stop = min(n, k + 1)
        s_start = k - (stop - 1)
        s_end = k - start + 1
        d[start:stop] += inv * a[s_start:s_end][::-1]

    return 2.0 * d


def add_start(starts, a, n):
    """Evaluate and append a candidate start if finite."""
    try:
        a = normalize_sequence(np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64), float(n))
        sc = fast_score_array(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            starts.append((float(sc), a))
    except Exception:
        pass


def make_two_block(n, frac, ratio):
    """Two-block sequence with left length frac*n and level ratio A/B."""
    n = int(n)
    m = int(round(frac * n))
    if m < 1:
        m = 1
    if m >= n:
        m = n - 1

    denom = m * float(ratio) + (n - m)
    if denom <= 0.0:
        B = 1.0
    else:
        B = float(n) / denom
    A = float(ratio) * B

    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[:m] = A
    a[m:] = B
    return normalize_sequence(a, float(n))


def make_harmonic_sequence(n, rng, max_freq=6, max_amp=2.0):
    """Random low-freq cosine/sine shape, exponentiated to stay positive."""
    n = int(n)
    if n < 2:
        return normalize_sequence(np.ones(n), float(n))

    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    z = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    for m in range(1, max_freq + 1):
        amp = rng.uniform(-max_amp, max_amp) / ((m + 1.0) ** 0.7)
        ph = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
        z += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * t + ph)

    amp = rng.uniform(-max_amp, max_amp)
    z += amp * np.cos(np.pi * (2.0 * t - 1.0))

    z -= float(np.max(z))
    a = np.exp(z)
    return normalize_sequence(a, float(n))


def make_block_sequence(n, rng, num_blocks=8):
    """Random piecewise-constant sequence."""
    n = int(n)
    num_blocks = int(max(2, min(num_blocks, n // 2)))
    if n < 4:
        return normalize_sequence(np.ones(n), float(n))

    edges = np.round(np.linspace(0.0, n, num_blocks + 1)).astype(int)
    edges[0] = 0
    edges[-1] = n
    lengths = np.diff(edges)

    if np.any(lengths <= 0):
        edges = np.linspace(0.0, n, num_blocks + 1).astype(int)
        edges[0] = 0
        edges[-1] = n
        lengths = np.diff(edges)

    vals = rng.uniform(0.1, 3.0, num_blocks)
    if rng.random() < 0.25:
        vals[int(rng.integers(0, num_blocks))] = 0.0

    a = np.repeat(vals, lengths)
    return normalize_sequence(a, float(n))


def upsample_sequence(seq, n):
    """Linearly interpolate a sequence to length n and renormalize."""
    seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    m = seq.size
    n = int(n)

    if m == 0:
        return normalize_sequence(np.ones(n), float(n))
    if m == n:
        return normalize_sequence(seq, float(n))

    xold = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    xnew = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    out = np.interp(xnew, xold, seq)
    return normalize_sequence(out, float(n))


def smooth_perturbation(a, rng, strength=0.35):
    """Multiplicative smooth perturbation used for restarts."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = a.size
    if n > 2:
        t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
        z = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for m in range(1, 4):
            amp = strength / (0.5 + 0.5 * m)
            ph = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
            z += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * t + ph)
        a *= np.exp(z)

    if n > 4 and rng.random() < 0.35:
        width = int(rng.integers(max(1, n // 200), max(2, n // 10 + 1)))
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        stop = min(n, start + width)
        a[start:stop] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, strength, stop - start))

    return normalize_sequence(a, float(n))


def generate_starts(n, rng, previous_best, random_count=30):
    """Create a diverse pool of start sequences for a given length."""
    n = int(n)
    starts = []

    add_start(starts, np.ones(n), n)

    # Deterministic two-block starts.
    for frac in (0.10, 0.20, 0.30, 0.40, 0.45):
        for ratio in (0.5, 0.8, 1.2, 2.0, 4.0):
            add_start(starts, make_two_block(n, frac, ratio), n)

    # Simple global shapes: ramps, quadratics, half-waves.
    if n > 1:
        t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
        for alpha in (-1.5, -1.0, -0.5, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5):
            add_start(starts, 1.0 + alpha * (2.0 * t - 1.0), n)
            add_start(starts, 1.0 + alpha * ((2.0 * t - 1.0) ** 2 - 1.0 / 3.0), n)
            add_start(starts, 1.0 + alpha * np.cos(np.pi * (2.0 * t - 1.0)), n)
            add_start(starts, 1.0 + alpha * np.sin(np.pi * t), n)

    # Random smooth/block starts.
    for _ in range(max(0, int(random_count))):
        if rng.random() < 0.5:
            nb = int(rng.integers(4, 17))
            a = make_block_sequence(n, rng, nb)
        else:
            a = make_harmonic_sequence(n, rng)
        add_start(starts, a, n)

    if previous_best is not None:
        add_start(starts, upsample_sequence(previous_best, n), n)

    try:
        parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
        if parent is not None and len(parent) > 0:
            add_start(starts, upsample_sequence(parent, n), n)
    except Exception:
        pass

    if not starts:
        starts.append((2.0, normalize_sequence(np.ones(n), float(n))))

    starts.sort(key=_pair_score)
    return starts


def local_search(a, budget_s, seed=0, label=""):
    """
    Multiplicative projected-gradient local search.

    The sequence is kept nonnegative and normalized to sum = n.
    The step direction is the top-peak subgradient, centered to preserve
    the sum constraint.  Momentum, adaptive step, noise, and occasional
    smooth restarts help escape flat/stagnant regions.
    """
    a = normalize_sequence(np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64), float(len(a)))
    n = int(a.size)
    target = float(n)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, float(budget_s))

    c = fast_convolution(a)
    s = float(a.sum())
    score = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(c)) / (s * s)) if s > 0 else float("inf")

    best = a.copy()
    best_score = score

    eta = 0.25
    beta = 0.35
    mom = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    accept_rel = 0.03
    noise = 0.02
    stagnant = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)) or float(a.sum()) <= 0.0:
            a = best.copy()
            c = fast_convolution(a)
            score = best_score
            mom = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

        L = 2 * n - 1
        frac = max(0.002, 0.04 / (1.0 + it / 2000.0))
        K = int(L * frac)
        K = max(1, min(K, min(512, max(1, n // 8))))

        grad = gradient_topK(a, c, K)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
            break

        g = grad - float(np.mean(grad))
        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
        if scale < 1e-14:
            stagnant += 1
            if stagnant > 80:
                break
            continue

        g /= scale
        mom = beta * mom + (1.0 - beta) * g

        logf = -eta * mom
        if noise > 1e-6:
            logf += rng.normal(0.0, noise, n)
        logf = np.clip(logf, -3.0, 3.0)

        cand = a * np.exp(logf)
        cand = np.maximum(cand, 0.0)
        cand = normalize_sequence(cand, target)

        if float(cand.max()) > 1000.0:
            cand = 0.5 * cand + 0.5 * best
            cand = normalize_sequence(cand, target)

        c2 = fast_convolution(cand)
        s2 = float(cand.sum())
        cand_score = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(c2)) / (s2 * s2)) if s2 > 0 else float("inf")

        if not np.isfinite(cand_score):
            eta = max(0.01, eta * 0.7)
            mom *= 0.5
            stagnant += 1
            continue

        if cand_score < best_score:
            best = cand.copy()
            best_score = cand_score
            stagnant = 0

        if cand_score <= score * (1.0 + accept_rel) + 1e-14:
            old = score
            a = cand
            c = c2
            score = cand_score
            mom *= 0.8

            if cand_score < old * (1.0 - 1e-10):
                eta = min(1.0, eta * 1.07)
                accept_rel = max(0.001, accept_rel * 0.998)
                noise = max(0.002, noise * 0.998)
            else:
                eta = max(0.03, eta * 0.96)

            stagnant += 1
        else:
            eta = max(0.01, eta * 0.7)
            mom *= 0.5
            stagnant += 1

        if stagnant > 800:
            a = smooth_perturbation(best, rng, 0.35)
            c = fast_convolution(a)
            s = float(a.sum())
            score = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(c)) / (s * s)) if s > 0 else float("inf")
            if (not np.isfinite(score)) or score > best_score * 1.05:
                a = best.copy()
                c = fast_convolution(a)
                score = best_score
            eta = 0.3
            accept_rel = 0.05
            noise = 0.05
            mom = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            stagnant = 0

    return best, best_score


def run_level(n, budget_s, previous_best, seed=0, label=""):
    """Generate starts for a length and locally refine the best ones."""
    n = int(n)
    budget_s = max(0.0, float(budget_s))
    if budget_s <= 5.0:
        seq = upsample_sequence(previous_best, n) if previous_best is not None else np.ones(n)
        seq = normalize_sequence(seq, float(n))
        return seq, fast_score_array(seq)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + budget_s

    starts = generate_starts(n, rng, previous_best, random_count=35)

    remain = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
    k = max(1, min(len(starts), int(remain / 35.0) + 1, 5))
    time_each = remain / float(k)

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    for i in range(k):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 0.5:
            break
        sc, sq = starts[i]
        if sc < best_score:
            best_seq = sq.copy()
            best_score = sc

        time_this = max(1.0, min(time_each, deadline - time.monotonic() - 0.5))
        if time_this <= 0.0:
            break

        try:
            seq, score = local_search(sq, time_this, seed=seed * 100 + i, label=label)
            if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
                best_seq = seq
                best_score = score
        except Exception:
            continue

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = normalize_sequence(np.ones(n), float(n))
        best_score = fast_score_array(best_seq)

    return best_seq, best_score


def add_candidate_pool(pool, seq, score):
    """Maintain a small pool of promising sequences."""
    if seq is None or score is None:
        return
    try:
        sc = float(score)
    except Exception:
        return
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return

    pool.append((sc, np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).copy()))
    pool.sort(key=_pair_score)
    if len(pool) > 12:
        pool.pop()


def sanitize_for_return(seq):
    """Return a valid Python list of non-negative finite floats."""
    seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    if seq.size == 0:
        return [1.0]

    seq = np.where(np.isfinite(seq), seq, 0.0)
    seq = np.clip(seq, 0.0, 1000.0)

    s = float(seq.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        return [1.0] * 1024

    # Rescale to mean 1 if this does not violate the 1000 clipping cap.
    scale = float(seq.size) / s
    if seq.max() * scale <= 1000.0:
        seq = seq * scale

    seq = np.clip(seq, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if float(seq.sum()) < 0.01:
        return [1.0] * 1024

    return [float(x) for x in seq]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Main search entry point.

    Uses a coarse-to-fine plan:
    1. Optimize at progressively larger lengths.
    2. Refine the best final-length candidate for the remaining time.
    3. Exact-evaluate a few top candidates and return the best valid one.
    """
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(0.0, float(budget_s))

    if budget < 25.0:
        return [1.0] * 1024

    # Leave time for exact evaluation and final cleanup.
    search_deadline = t0 + max(10.0, budget - 45.0)

    if budget < 300.0:
        final_n = 1024
        small_levels = []
    elif budget < 900.0:
        final_n = 2048
        small_levels = [256, 512, 1024]
    else:
        final_n = 6733
        small_levels = [256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096]

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    candidates = []
    global_best_seq = None
    global_best_score = float("inf")

    tiny_fallback = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64)
    add_candidate_pool(candidates, tiny_fallback, 2.0)

    # If budget is modest, skip small levels and go directly to final length.
    if small_levels and (search_deadline - time.monotonic()) > 180.0:
        weights = [1.0, 1.4, 2.2, 3.5, 5.5]
        small_budget = min(600.0, 0.25 * max(0.0, search_deadline - time.monotonic()))
        if small_levels[-1] < final_n:
            small_budget = min(small_budget, 0.25 * max(0.0, search_deadline - time.monotonic()))
        else:
            small_budget = 0.0

        scale = small_budget / float(sum(weights[:len(small_levels)])) if small_levels else 0.0
        prev_best = None

        for idx, level_n in enumerate(small_levels):
            b = scale * weights[idx]
            if b < 12.0 or time.monotonic() >= search_deadline - 30.0:
                break
            try:
                seq, sc = run_level(
                    level_n,
                    b,
                    prev_best,
                    seed=int(seed + 1000 + idx),
                    label="small"
                )
            except Exception:
                seq, sc = None, float("inf")

            if seq is not None and np.isfinite(sc) and sc < global_best_score:
                global_best_seq = seq
                global_best_score = sc
                add_candidate_pool(candidates, seq, sc)
                prev_best = seq

    # Final length start pool.
    try:
        final_starts = generate_starts(final_n, rng, global_best_seq, random_count=45)
    except Exception:
        final_starts = [(2.0, normalize_sequence(np.ones(final_n), float(final_n)))]

    for sc, sq in final_starts[:5]:
        if sc < global_best_score:
            global_best_seq = sq.copy()
            global_best_score = sc
            add_candidate_pool(candidates, sq, sc)

    # Quick refinement of a few final-length starts.
    remain = max(0.0, search_deadline - time.monotonic())
    quick_budget = min(420.0, 0.25 * remain)
    if quick_budget > 30.0 and final_starts:
        k = max(1, min(4, len(final_starts), int(quick_budget / 80.0) + 1))
        time_each = quick_budget / float(k)
        for i in range(k):
            if time.monotonic() >= search_deadline - 45.0:
                break
            sc, sq = final_starts[i]
            time_this = max(10.0, min(time_each, search_deadline - time.monotonic() - 45.0))
            try:
                seq, score = local_search(
                    sq,
                    time_this,
                    seed=int(seed + 7000 + i),
                    label="final_quick"
                )
                if seq is not None and score < global_best_score:
                    global_best_score = score
                    global_best_seq = seq
                    add_candidate_pool(candidates, seq, score)
            except Exception:
                continue

    # Long refinement on the current global best.
    long_budget = max(0.0, search_deadline - time.monotonic())
    if long_budget > 30.0 and global_best_seq is not None:
        try:
            seq, score = local_search(
                global_best_seq,
                long_budget,
                seed=int(seed + 12345),
                label="final_long"
            )
            if seq is not None and score < global_best_score:
                global_best_score = score
                global_best_seq = seq
                add_candidate_pool(candidates, seq, score)
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Ensure there is at least something valid.
    final_fallback = normalize_sequence(np.ones(final_n), float(final_n))
    add_candidate_pool(candidates, final_fallback, fast_score_array(final_fallback))

    if global_best_seq is None:
        global_best_seq = final_fallback
        global_best_score = fast_score_array(global_best_seq)

    # Exact validation of a few best fast-score candidates.
    exact_best_seq = global_best_seq
    exact_best_score = float(np.float64(global_best_score)) if np.isfinite(global_best_score) else float("inf")

    candidates.sort(key=_pair_score)
    top_exact = candidates[:4]
    for i in range(len(top_exact)):
        if time.monotonic() > search_deadline:
            break
        _, seq = top_exact[i]
        try:
            ex = float(evaluate_sequence(seq.tolist()))
        except Exception:
            ex = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(ex) and ex < exact_best_score:
            exact_best_score = ex
            exact_best_seq = seq

    if exact_best_seq is None or (not np.isfinite(exact_best_score)):
        exact_best_seq = final_fallback

    return sanitize_for_return(exact_best_seq)
```
